OUTPUT: set turn="review", fill the "review" object, and set every other section to null.
REVIEW TURN. Independently audit EVERY candidate in `candidates`; each is a
hypothesis from the discovery turn, not a trusted conclusion. Return exactly one
decision per candidate_id.

Return obligation_class (execution_compatibility per D4, target_coverage per D5),
scope_basis, trigger_basis and the five checks of J1, each with status
established/refuted/unknown, a specific reason and exact evidence:
scope_contract (J1a, D2); feasible_trigger (J1b): for execution compatibility a
finite actual target execution, for target coverage the changed code together
with the build, setup or input framing that would execute it and that the target
cannot produce; observable_consequence (J1c);
alternatives_ruled_out (J1d, D8), including counterevidence supplied with the
candidate; identity_preserved (J1e; for timing also T1).

Use effect blocked_target_coverage only for target_coverage. For target_coverage
fill every coverage_witness field; for the other class use empty strings. These
fields do not replace the citations of each check.

decision=keep requires all five checks established with source and harness
evidence, a finite_witness and no decisive missing fact. decision=dismiss requires
a grounded refutation or equivalent route (J6). Otherwise return uncertain and
request the missing facts (E3). For maintenance, unresolved timing does not
invalidate a currently established obligation (J2).

Then audit the whole scope again (J4): assess every selected change ID and the
four foundations with evidence, challenge both dismissals (J6) and retained
gaps (J1). Out-of-scope items (D7) are not obligations. New evidence contains
contracts retrieved for earlier requests (`evidence`); `citation_errors` lists earlier
citations the program could not verify; cite valid locations instead. Request
exact symbols or paths only for decisive missing facts (E3).
If the evidence establishes a distinct obligation that is NOT among `candidates`
(for example one that only became visible with the new evidence), report it in
`new_candidates` with the same fields as a discovery blocker and mark its change
group as blocked in the scope audit. It is reviewed in a separate turn; do not
add a decision for it here.

scripts/pnglibconf.dfa - library build configuration control\n2|#\n3|@/*- pnglibconf.dfn intermediate file\n4|@ *  generated from scripts/pnglibconf.dfa\n5|@ */\n6|#\n7|com pnglibconf.h - library build configuration\n8|com\n9|version\n10|com\n11|com Copyright (c) 1998-2017 Glenn Randers-Pehrson\n12|com\n13|com This code is released under the libpng license.\n14|com For conditions of distribution and use, see the disclaimer\n15|com and license in png.h\n16|com\n17|\n18|file pnglibconf.h scripts/pnglibconf.dfa PNGLCONF_H\n19|\n20|# This file is preprocessed by scripts/options.awk and the\n21|# C compiler to generate 'pnglibconf.h' - a list of all the\n22|# configuration options.  The file lists the various options\n23|# that can *only* be specified during the libpng build;\n24|# pnglibconf.h freezes the definitions selected for the specific\n25|# build.\n26|#\n27|# The syntax is detailed in scripts/options.awk; this is a summary\n28|# only:\n29|#\n30|# setting <name> [requires ...] [default]\n31|#    #define PNG_<name> <value>  /* value comes from current setting */\n32|# option <name> [requires ...] [if ...] [enables ...] [disabled]\n33|#    #define PNG_<name>_SUPPORTED if the requirements are met and\n34|#    enable the other options listed\n35|# chunk <name> [requires ...] [enables ...] [disabled]\n36|#    Enable chunk processing for the given ancillary chunk; any\n37|#    'requires something' expands to READ_something for read and\n38|#    WRITE_something for write, but the enables list members are\n39|#    used as given (e.g. enables GAMMA just expands to that on the\n40|#    correspond READ_name and WRITE_name lines.)\n41|#\n42|# \",\" may be used to separate options on an 'option' line and is ignored; it\n43|# doesn't change the meaning of the line.  (NOT setting, where \",\" becomes\n44|# part of the setting!)  A comma at the end of an option line causes a\n45|# continuation (the next line is included in the option too.)\n46|#\n47|# Note that the 'on' and 'off' keywords, while valid on both option\n48|# and chunk, should not be used in this file because they force the\n49|# relevant options on or off.\n50|\n51|#----------------------------------------------------------------------\n52|\n53|# The following setting, option and chunk values can all be changed\n54|# while building libpng:\n55|#\n56|# setting: change 'setting' lines to fine tune library performance;\n57|#   changes to the settings don't affect the libpng API functionally\n58|#\n59|# option: change 'option' lines to remove or add capabilities from\n60|#   or to the library; options change the library API\n61|#\n62|# chunk: change 'chunk' lines to remove capabilities to process\n63|#   optional ('ancillary') chunks.  This does not prevent PNG\n64|#   decoding but does change the libpng API because some chunks\n65|#   will be ignored.\n66|#\n67|# There are three ways of disabling features, in no particular order:\n68|#\n69|# 1) Create 'pngusr.h', enter the required private build information\n70|# detailed below and #define PNG_NO_<option> for each option you\n71|# don't want in that file in that file.  You can also turn on options\n72|# using PNG_<option>_SUPPORTED.  When you have finished rerun\n73|# configure and rebuild pnglibconf.h file with -DPNG_USER_CONFIG:\n74|#\n75|#  make clean\n76|#  CPPFLAGS='-DPNG_USER_CONFIG' ./configure\n77|#  make pnglibconf.h\n78|#\n79|# pngusr.h is only used during the creation of pnglibconf.h, but it\n80|# is safer to ensure that -DPNG_USER_CONFIG is specified throughout\n81|# the build by changing the CPPFLAGS passed to the initial ./configure\n82|#\n83|# 2) Add definitions of the settings you want to change to\n84|# CPPFLAGS; for example:\n85|#\n86|#   -DPNG_DEFAULT_READ_MACROS=0\n87|#\n88|# (This would change the default to *not* use read macros.)  Be\n89|# very careful to change only settings that don't alter the API\n90|# because this approach bypasses the private build checking.  You\n91|# can also change settings from pngpriv.h (read pngpriv.h) safely\n92|# without API changes.  Do that in the same way.\n93|#\n94|# 3) Write a new '.dfa' file (say 'pngusr.dfa') and in this file\n95|# provide override values for setting entries and turn option or\n96|# chunk values explicitly 'on' or 'off':\n97|#\n98|#    setting FOO default VALUE\n99|#    option BAR [on|off]\n100|#\n101|# Then add this file to the options.awk command line (the *first*\n102|# one) after this file.  The make macro DFA_XTRA is provided to make\n103|# this easier (set it like CPPFLAGS prior to running ./configure).\n104|# Look at the builds below contrib/pngminim for some extreme examples\n105|# of how this can be used.\n106|#\n107|# Don't edit this file unless you are contributing a patch to\n108|# libpng and need new or modified options/settings.\n109|#----------------------------------------------------------------------\n110|\n111|# The following causes commented out #undef lines to be written to\n112|# pnglibconf.h; this can be stopped by logunsupported=0 in a later\n113|# file or on the command line (after pnglibconf.dfa)\n114|\n115|logunsupported = 1\n116|\n117|# The following allows the output from configure to modify the contents of\n118|# pnglibconf.h\n119|\n120|@#ifdef HAVE_CONFIG_H\n121|@#  include \"config.h\"\n122|@#endif\n123|\n124|# PNG_USER_CONFIG has to be defined on the compiler command line\n125|# to cause pngusr.h to be read while constructing pnglibconf.h\n126|#\n127|# If you create a private DLL you need to define the following\n128|# macros in the file 'pngusr.h' and set -DPNG_USER_CONFIG for\n129|# compilation (i.e. in CPPFLAGS.)\n130|# #define PNG_USER_PRIVATEBUILD \\\n131|#     <Describes by whom and why this version of the DLL was built>\n132|#  e.g. #define PNG_USER_PRIVATEBUILD \"Build by MyCompany for xyz reasons.\"\n133|# #define PNG_USER_DLLFNAME_POSTFIX <two-letter postfix that serve to\n134|#        distinguish your DLL from those of the official release. These\n135|#        correspond to the trailing letters that come after the version\n136|#        number and must match your private DLL name>\n137|#  e.g. // private DLL \"libpng13gx.dll\"\n138|#       #define PNG_USER_DLLFNAME_POSTFIX \"gx\"\n139|#\n140|# The following macros are also at your disposal if you want to complete the\n141|# DLL VERSIONINFO structure.\n142|# - PNG_USER_VERSIONINFO_COMMENTS\n143|# - PNG_USER_VERSIONINFO_COMPANYNAME\n144|# - PNG_USER_VERSIONINFO_LEGALTRADEMARKS\n145|\n146|# It is necessary to include configures definitions here so that AC_DEFINE\n147|# in configure.ac works in a comprehensible way\n148|@#if defined(HAVE_CONFIG_H) && !defined(PNG_NO_CONFIG_H)\n149|@#  include \"config.h\"\n150|@#endif\n151|\n152|@#ifdef PNG_USER_CONFIG\n153|@#  include \"pngusr.h\"\n154|@#endif\n155|\n156|# This is a special fixup for the Watcom C compiler on Windows, which has\n157|# multiple procedure call standards.  Unless PNG_API_RULE is set explicitly\n158|# (i.e. if it is not defined at this point) it will be forced to '2' here when\n159|# using Watcom.  This indicates to the other header files that Watcom behaviour\n160|# is required where appropriate.\n161|\n162|@#ifdef __WATCOMC__\n163|@#  ifndef PNG_API_RULE\n164|@#     define PNG_API_RULE 2 /* Use Watcom calling conventions */\n165|@#  endif\n166|@#endif\n167|\n168|# IN DEVELOPMENT\n169|# These are currently experimental features; define them if you want (NOTE:\n170|# experimental options must be disabled before they are defined in this file!)\n171|\n172|# NONE\n173|\n174|# Note that PNG_USER_CONFIG only has an effect when building\n175|# pnglibconf.h\n176|\n177|setting USER_CONFIG\n178|setting USER_PRIVATEBUILD\n179|setting USER_DLLFNAME_POSTFIX\n180|setting USER_VERSIONINFO_COMMENTS\n181|setting USER_VERSIONINFO_COMPANYNAME\n182|setting USER_VERSIONINFO_LEGALTRADEMARKS\n183|\n184|# Record the 'API rule' used to select calling conventions on\n185|# those systems that support such things (see all the comments in\n186|# pngconf.h)\n187|# Changing this setting has a fundamental affect on the PNG ABI,\n188|# do not release shared libraries with this changed.\n189|\n190|setting API_RULE default 0\n191|\n192|# This allows a prefix to be added to the front of every API functon name (and\n193|# therefore every symbol) by redefining all the function names with the prefix\n194|# at the end of pnglibconf.h.  It also turns on similar internal symbol renaming\n195|# by causing a similar build-time only file, pngprefix.h, to be generated.\n196|\n197|setting PREFIX\n198|\n199|# Implementation specific control of the optimizations, enabled by those\n200|# hardware or software options that need it (typically when run-time choices\n201|# must be made by the user)\n202|option SET_OPTION disabled\n203|\n204|# These options are specific to the ARM NEON hardware optimizations.  At present\n205|# these optimizations depend on GCC specific pre-processing of an assembler (.S)\n206|# file so they probably won't work with other compilers.\n207|#\n208|# ARM_NEON_OPT: unset: check at compile time (__ARM_NEON__ must be defined by\n209|#                      the compiler, typically as a result of specifying\n210|#                      CC=\"gcc -mfpu=neon\".)\n211|#                   0: disable (even if the CPU has a NEON FPU.)\n212|#                   1: check at run time (via ARM_NEON_{API,CHECK})\n213|#                   2: switch on unconditionally (inadvisable - instead pass\n214|#                      -mfpu=neon to GCC in CC)\n215|#           When building libpng avoid using any setting other than '0'; '1' is\n216|#           set automatically when either 'API' or 'CHECK' are configured in,\n217|#           '2' should not be necessary as -mfpu=neon will achieve the same\n218|#           effect as well as applying NEON optimizations to the rest of the\n219|#           libpng code.\n220|#           NOTE: any setting other than '0' requires ALIGNED_MEMORY\n221|# ARM_NEON_API:   (PNG_ARM_NEON == 1) allow the optimization to be switched on\n222|#                 with png_set_option\n223|# ARM_NEON_CHECK: (PNG_ARM_NEON == 1) compile a run-time check to see if Neon\n224|#                 extensions are supported. This is poorly supported and\n225|#                 deprecated - use the png_set_option API.\n226|setting ARM_NEON_OPT\n227|option ARM_NEON_API disabled requires ALIGNED_MEMORY enables SET_OPTION,\n228|   sets ARM_NEON_OPT 1\n229|option ARM_NEON_CHECK disabled requires ALIGNED_MEMORY,\n230|   sets ARM_NEON_OPT 1\n231|\n232|# These options are specific to the PowerPC VSX hardware optimizations.\n233|#\n234|# POWERPC_VSX_OPT: unset: check at compile time (__PPC64__,__ALTIVEC__,__VSX__\n235|#                      must be defined by the compiler, typically as a result\n236|#                      of specifying\n237|#                      \"-mvsx -maltivec\" compiler flags)\n238|#                   0: disable (even if the CPU supports VSX.)\n239|#                   1: check at run time (via POWERPC_VSX_{API,CHECK})\n240|#                   2: switch on unconditionally (inadvisable - instead pass\n241|#                      -mvsx -maltivec to compiler options)\n242|#           When building libpng avoid using any setting other than '0'; '1' is\n243|#           set automatically when either 'API' or 'CHECK' are configured in,\n244|#           '2' should not be necessary as \"-mvsx -maltivec\" will achieve the same\n245|#           effect as well as applying VSX optimizations to the rest of the\n246|#           libpng code.\n247|# POWERPC_VSX_API:   (PNG_POWERPC_VSX == 1) allow the optimization to be switched on\n248|#                 with png_set_option\n249|# POWERPC_VSX_CHECK: (PNG_POWERPC_VSX == 1) compile a run-time check to see if VSX\n250|#                 extensions are supported. This is supported not for all OSes\n251|#                 (see contrib/powerpc/README)\n252|setting POWERPC_VSX_OPT\n253|option POWERPC_VSX_API disabled enables SET_OPTION,\n254|  sets POWERPC_VSX_OPT 1\n255|option POWERPC_VSX_CHECK disabled,\n256|  sets POWERPC_VSX_OPT 1\n257|\n258|\n259|# These settings configure the default compression level (0-9) and 'strategy';\n260|# strategy is as defined by the implementors of zlib. It describes the input\n261|# data and modifies the zlib parameters in an attempt to optimize the balance\n262|# between search and huffman encoding in the zlib algorithms.  The defaults are\n263|# the zlib.h defaults - the apparently recursive definition does not arise\n264|# because the name of the setting is prefixed by PNG_\n265|#\n266|# The TEXT values are the defaults when writing compressed text (all forms)\n267|\n268|# Include the zlib header so that the defaults below are known\n269|@#  include <zlib.h>\n270|\n271|# The '@' here means to substitute the value when pnglibconf.h is built\n272|setting Z_DEFAULT_COMPRESSION default @Z_DEFAULT_COMPRESSION\n273|# TODO: why aren't these Z_RLE; zlib.h says that Z_RLE, specifically, is\n274|# appropriate for PNG images, maybe it doesn't exist in all versions?\n275|setting Z_DEFAULT_STRATEGY default @Z_FILTERED\n276|setting Z_DEFAULT_NOFILTER_STRATEGY default @Z_DEFAULT_STRATEGY\n277|setting ZLIB_VERNUM default @ZLIB_VERNUM\n278|\n279|# Linkage of:\n280|#\n281|#  API:      libpng API functions\n282|#  CALLBACK: internal non-file-local callbacks\n283|#  FUNCTION: internal non-file-local functions\n284|#  DATA:     internal non-file-local (const) data\n285|setting LINKAGE_API default extern\n286|setting LINKAGE_CALLBACK default extern\n287|setting LINKAGE_FUNCTION default extern\n288|setting LINKAGE_DATA default extern\n289|\n290|setting TEXT_Z_DEFAULT_COMPRESSION default @Z_DEFAULT_COMPRESSION\n291|setting TEXT_Z_DEFAULT_STRATEGY default @Z_DEFAULT_STRATEGY\n292|\n293|# Default to using the read macros\n294|\n295|setting DEFAULT_READ_MACROS default 1\n296|\n297|# The alternative is to call functions to read PNG values, if\n298|# the functions are turned *off* the read macros must always\n299|# be enabled, so turning this off will actually force the\n300|# USE_READ_MACROS option on (see pngconf.h)\n301|\n302|option READ_INT_FUNCTIONS requires READ\n303|\n304|# The same for write  but these can only be switched off if no writing\n305|# is required at all - hence the use of a 'disabled', not a 'requires'.\n306|# If these are needed, they are enabled in the 'WRITE options' section\n307|# below.\n308|\n309|option WRITE_INT_FUNCTIONS disabled\n310|\n311|# Error controls\n312|#\n313|# WARNINGS: normally on, if off no warnings are generated\n314|# ERROR_TEXT: normally on, if off errors happen but there is no message\n315|# ERROR_NUMBERS: unimplemented feature, therefore disabled\n316|# BENIGN_ERRORS: support for just issuing warnings for recoverable errors\n317|#\n318|# BENIGN_READ_ERRORS:\n319|#     By default recoverable errors on read should just generate warnings,\n320|#     generally safe but PNG files that don't conform to the specification will\n321|#     be accepted if a meaningful result can be produced.\n322|#\n323|# BENIGN_WRITE_ERRORS:\n324|#     By default recoverable errors on write should just generate warnings,\n325|#     not generally safe because this allows the application to write invalid\n326|#     PNG files.  Applications should enable this themselves; it's useful\n327|#     because it means that a failure to write an ancilliary chunk can often be\n328|#     ignored.\n329|\n330|option WARNINGS\n331|option ERROR_TEXT\n332|option ERROR_NUMBERS disabled\n333|\n334|option BENIGN_ERRORS\n335|option BENIGN_WRITE_ERRORS requires BENIGN_ERRORS disabled\n336|option BENIGN_READ_ERRORS requires BENIGN_ERRORS\n337|\n338|\n339|# Generic options - affect both read and write.\n340|\n341|option MNG_FEATURES\n342|\n343|# Arithmetic options, the first is the big switch that chooses between internal\n344|# floating and fixed point arithmetic implementations - it does not affect any\n345|# APIs. (Note: the list of chunks follows\n591|# the library-only settings.)\n592|#\n593|# BUILD TIME ONLY OPTIONS\n594|#   These options do not affect the API but rather alter how the\n595|#   API is implemented, they get recorded in pnglibconf.h, but\n596|#   can't be changed by the application.\n597|\n598|# Colorspace support (enabled as required); just the support for colorant\n599|# information.  Gamma support, likewise, is just support for the gamma\n600|# information, READ_GAMMA is required for gamma transformations (so it\n601|# is possible to read PNG gamma without enabling all the libpng transform\n602|# code - do this for applications that do their own gamma processing)\n603|#\n604|# As of 1.6.0 COLORSPACE is only useful if the application processes the\n605|# information; this is because the library does not do any colorspace\n606|# processing, it just validates the data in the PNG file.\n607|\n608|option GAMMA disabled\n609|option COLORSPACE enables GAMMA disabled\n610|\n611|# When an ICC profile is read, or png_set, it will be checked for a match\n612|# against known sRGB profiles if the sRGB handling is enabled.  The\n613|# PNG_sRGB_PROFILE_CHECKS setting controls how much work is done during the\n614|# check:\n615|#\n616|# -1: Don't do any sRGB profile checking.\n617|#\n618|#  0: Just validate the profile MD5 signature if present, otherwise use\n619|#     the checks in option 1.\n620|#\n621|#  1: Additionally check the length, intent and adler32 checksum of the\n622|#     actual data.   If enabled this will reject known profiles that have\n623|#     had the rendering intent in the header changed as well as other edits\n624|#     done without updating the checksum.  See the discussion below.\n625|#\n626|#  2: Additionally checksum all the data using the ethernet CRC32 algorithm.\n627|#     This makes it more difficult to fake profiles and makes it less likely\n628|#     to get a false positive on profiles with no signature, but is probably\n629|#     just a waste of time since all currently approved ICC sRGB profiles have\n630|#     a secure MD5 signature.\n631|#\n632|# The rendering intent.  An ICC profile stores an intended rendering intent,\n633|# but does not include the value in the signature.  The intent is documented\n634|# as the intent that should be used when combining two profiles.  The sRGB\n635|# profile is intended, however, to be used with any of the four defined intents.\n636|# For this reason the sRGB chunk includes an 'intent' to be used when displaying\n637|# the image (intent is really a property of the image not the profile.)\n638|#\n639|# Unfortunately the iCCP chunk does not.  It may therefore be that some\n640|# applications modify the intent in profiles (including sRGB profiles) to work\n641|# round this problem.  Selecting an option other than option '0' will cause such\n642|# modified profiles to be rejected.\n643|#\n644|# Security.  The use of Adler32 and CRC32 checksums does not help significantly\n645|# with any security issues.  It is relatively easy to produce arbitrary profiles\n646|# with the required checksums on current computer systems.  Nevertheless\n647|# security does not seem to be an issue because the only consequence of a false\n648|# positive is a false assertion that the profile is an sRGB profile.  This might\n649|# be used to hide data from libpng using applications, but it doesn't seem\n650|# possible to damage them.\n651|\n652|setting sRGB_PROFILE_CHECKS default 2\n653|\n654|# Artificially align memory - the code typically aligns to 8 byte\n655|# boundaries if this is switched on, it's a small waste of space\n656|# but can help (in theory) on some architectures. -f\n2|# scripts/options.awk - library build configuration control\n3|#\n4|# last changed in libpng version 1.6.11 - June 5, 2014\n5|#\n6|# Copyright (c) 1998-2014 Glenn Randers-Pehrson\n7|#\n8|# This by\n13|# the variable 'out'.  The script is run twice, once with\n14|# an intermediate output file, 'options.tmp' then again on\n15|# that file to produce the final output:\n16|#\n17|#  awk -f scripts/options.awk out=options.tmp scripts/options.dfa 1>&2\n18|#  awk -f scripts/options.awk out=options.dfn options.tmp 1>&2\n19|#\n20|# Some options may be specified on the command line:\n21|#\n22|#  deb=1            Causes debugging to be output\n23|#  logunsupported=1 Causes all options to be recorded in the output\n24|#  everything=off   Causes all options to be disabled by default\n25|#  everything=on    Causes all options to be enabled by default\n26|#\n27|# If awk fails on your platform, try nawk instead.\n28|#\n29|# These options may also be specified in the original input file (and\n30|# are copied to the preprocessed file).\n31|\n32|BEGIN{\n33|   out=\"\"                       # intermediate, preprocessed, file\n34|   pre=-1                       # preprocess (first line)\n35|   version=\"libpng version unknown\" # version information\n36|   version_file=\"\"              # where to find the version\n37|   err=0                        # in-line exit sets this\n38|   # The following definitions prevent the C preprocessor noticing the lines\n39|   # that will be in the final output file.  Some C preprocessors tokenise\n40|   # the lines, for example by inserting spaces around operators, and all\n41|   # C preprocessors notice lines that start with '#', most remove comments.\n42|   # The technique adopted here is to make the final output lines into\n43|   # C strings (enclosed in double quotes), preceeded by PNG_DFN.  As a\n44|   # consequence the output cannot contain a 'raw' double quote - instead put\n45|   # @' in, this will be replaced by a single \" afterward.  See the parser\n46|   # script dfn.awk for more capabilities (not required here).  Note that if\n47|   # you need a \" in a 'setting' in pnglibconf.dfa it must also be @'!\n48|   dq=\"@'\"                      # For a single double quote\n49|   start=\" PNG_DFN \\\"\"          # Start stuff to output (can't contain a \"!)\n50|   end=\"\\\" \"                    # Comment end\n55|   def=start \"#define PNG_\"     # Arbitrary define\n56|   sup=\"_SUPPORTED\" end         # end supported option\n57|   und=comment \"#undef PNG_\"    # Unsupported option\n58|   une=\"_SUPPORTED\" cend        # And fall through to continue processing\n103|   }\n104|}\n105|\n106|# While pre-processing if version is set to \"search\" look for a version string\n107|# in the following file.\n108|pre && version == \"search\" && version_file == \"\"{\n109|   version_file = FILENAME\n110|}\n111|\n112|pre && version == \"search\" && version_file != FILENAME{\n113|   print \"version string not found in\", version_file\n114|   err = 1\n115|   exit 1\n116|}\n117|\n118|pre && version == \"search\" && $0 ~ /^ \\* libpng version/{\n119|   version = substr($0, 4)\n120|   print \"version =\", version >out\n121|   next\n122|}\n123|\n124|pre && FILENAME == version_file{\n125|   next\n126|}\n127|\n128|# variable=value\n129|#   Sets the given variable to the given value (the syntax is fairly\n130|#   free form, except for deb (you are expected to understand how to\n131|#   set the debug variable...)\n132|#\n133|#   This happens before the check on 'pre' below skips most of the\n134|#   rest of the actions, so the variable settings happen during\n135|#   preprocessing but are recorded in the END action too.  This\n136|#   allows them to be set on the command line too.\n137|$0 ~ /^[ \t]*version[ \t]*=/{\n138|   sub(/^[  ]*version[  ]*=[  ]*/, \"\")\n139|   version = $0\n140|   next\n141|}\n142|$0 ~ /^[ \t]*everything[ \t=]*off[ \t]*$/{\n143|   everything = \"off\"\n144|   next\n145|}\n146|$0 ~ /^[ \t]*everything[ \t=]*on[ \t]*$/{\n147|   everything = \"on\"\n148|   next\n149|}\n150|$0 ~ /^[ \t]*logunsupported[ \t=]*0[ \t]*$/{\n151|   logunsupported = 0\n152|   next\n153|}\n154|$0 ~ /^[ \t]*logunsupported[ \t=]*1[ \t]*$/{\n155|   logunsupported = 1\n156|   next\n157|}\n158|$1 == \"deb\" && $2 == \"=\" && NF == 3{\n159|   deb = $3\n160|   next\n161|}\n162|\n163|# Preprocessing - this just copies the input file with lines\n164|# that need preprocessing (just chunk at present) expanded\n165|# The bare \"pre\" instead of \"pre != 0\" crashes under Sunos awk\n166|pre && $1 != \"chunk\"{\n167|   print >out\n168|   next\n169|}\n170|\n171|# The first characters of the line determine how it is processed,\n172|# leading spaces are ignored.  In general tokens that are not\n173|# keywords are the names of options.  An option 'name' is\n174|# controlled by the definition of the corresponding macros:\n175|#\n176|#   PNG_name_SUPPORTED    The option is turned on\n177|#   PNG_NO_name\n178|#   PNG_NO_name_SUPPORTED If the first macro is not defined\n179|#                         either of these will turn the option off\n180|#\n181|# If none of these macros are defined the option is turned on, unless\n182|# the keyword 'off' is given in a line relating to the option. 'requires' and 'if' entries take precedence over\n451|# 'enables' from other options; if an option requires another option it\n452|# won't be set regardless of any options that enable it unless the other\n453|# option is also enabled.\n454|#\n455|# Similarly 'enables' trumps a NO_ definition in CFLAGS or pngusr.h\n456|#\n457|# For simplicity cycles in the definitions are regarded as errors,\n458|# even if they are not ambiguous.\n459|# A given NAME can be specified in as many 'option' lines as required, the\n460|# definitions are additive.\n461|\n462|# For backwards compatibility equivalent macros may be listed thus:\n463|#\n464|# = [NO_]NAME MACRO\n465|#   Makes -DMACRO equivalent to -DPNG_NO_NAME or -DPNG_NAME_SUPPORTED\n466|#   as appropriate.\n467|#\n468|# The definition is injected into the C compiler input when encountered\n469|# in the second pass (so all these definitions appear *after* the @\n470|# lines!)\n471|#\n472|# 'NAME' is as above, but 'MACRO' is the full text of the equivalent\n473|# old, deprecated, macro.\n474|\n475|$1 == \"=\" && NF == 3{\n476|   print \"#ifdef PNG_\" $3 >out\n477|   if ($2 ~ /^NO_/)\n478|      print \"#   define PNG_\" $2 >out\n479|   else\n480|      print \"#   define PNG_\" $2 \"_SUPPORTED\" >out\n481|   print \"#endif\" >out\n482|   next\n483|}\n484|\n485|# Lines may be injected into the C compiler input by preceding them\n486|# with an \"@\" character. This allows interdependencies\n508|# between options of the form \"at least one of\" or \"at most one of\"\n509|# to be checked. Such comparisons are not permitted in\n25| * ANSI C90, however GCC isn't clever enough to work out that that do not occur\n26| * below in png_ascii_from_fp and png_muldiv, so it produces a warning with\n27| * -Wextra. We allow\n66| * checking less than the full 8-byte signature so that those apps that\n67| * already read the first few bytes of a file to determine the file type\n68| * can simply check the remaining bytes for extra assurance.  Returns\n69| * an integer less than, equal to, or greater than zero if sig is found,\n70| * respectively, to be less than, to match, or be greater than the correct\n71| * PNG signature (this is the same behavior as strcmp, memcmp, etc).\n72| */\n73|int PNGAPI\n74|png_sig_cmp(png_const_bytep sig, png_size_t start, png_size_t num_to_check)\n75|{\n76|   png_byte png_signature[8] = {137, 80, 78, 71, 13, 10, 26, 10};\n77|\n78|   if (num_to_check > 8)\n79|      num_to_check = 8;\n80|\n81|   else if (num_to_check < 1)\n82|      return (-1);\n83|\n84|   if (start > 7)\n85|      return (-1);\n86|\n87|   if (start + num_to_check > 8)\n88|      num_to_check = 8 - start;\n89|\n90|   return ((int)(memcmp(&sig[start], &png_signature[start], num_to_check)));\n91|}\n92|\n93|#endif /* READ */\n94|\n95|#if defined(PNG_READ_SUPPORTED) || defined(PNG_WRITE_SUPPORTED)\n96|/* Function to allocate memory for zlib */\n97|PNG_FUNCTION(voidpf /* PRIVATE */,\n98|png_zalloc,(voidpf png_ptr, uInt items, uInt size),PNG_ALLOCATED)\n99|{\n100|   png_alloc_size_t num_bytes = size;\n101|\n102|   if (png_ptr == NULL)\n103|      return NULL;\n104|\n105|   if (items >= (~(png_alloc_size_t)0)/size)\n106|   {\n107|      png_warning (png_voidcast(png_structrp, png_ptr),\n108|          \"Potential overflow in png_zalloc()\");\n109|      return NULL;\n110|   }\n111|\n112|   num_bytes *= items;\n113|   return png_malloc_warn(png_voidcast(png_structrp, png_ptr), num_bytes);\n114|}\n115|\n116|/* Function to free memory for zlib */\n117|void /* PRIVATE */\n118|png_zfree(voidpf png_ptr, voidpf ptr)\n119|{\n120|   png_free(png_voidcast(png_const_structrp,png_ptr), ptr);\n121|}\n122|\n123|/* Reset the CRC variable to 32 bits of 1's. We can only pass as\n134| * much data to this routine as the largest single buffer size.  We\n135| * also check that this data will actually be used before going to the\n136| * trouble of calculating it.\n137| */\n138|void /* PRIVATE */\n139|png_calculate_crc(png_structrp png_ptr, png_const_bytep ptr, png_size_t length)\n140|{\n141|   int need_crc = 1;\n142|\n143|   if (PNG_CHUNK_ANCILLARY(png_ptr->chunk_name) != 0)\n144|   {\n145|      if ((png_ptr->flags & PNG_FLAG_CRC_ANCILLARY_MASK) ==\n146|          (PNG_FLAG_CRC_ANCILLARY_USE | PNG_FLAG_CRC_ANCILLARY_NOWARN))\n147|         need_crc = 0;\n148|   }\n149|\n150|   else /* critical */\n151|   {\n152|      if ((png_ptr->flags & PNG_FLAG_CRC_CRITICAL_IGNORE) != 0)\n153|         need_crc = 0;\n154|   }\n155|\n156|   /* 'uLong' is defined in zlib.h as unsigned long; this means that on some\n157|    * systems it is a 64-bit value.  crc32, however, returns 32 bits so the\n158|    * following cast is safe. The X, Y and Z values are required to be\n1236| * positive and less than 1.0.\n1237| */\n1238|static int\n1239|png_xy_from_XYZ(png_xy *xy, const png_XYZ *XYZ)\n1240|{\n1241|   png_int_32 d, dwhite, whiteX, whiteY;\n1242|\n1243|   d = XYZ->red_X + XYZ->red_Y + XYZ->red_Z;\n1244|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->redx, XYZ->red_X, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1245|      return 1;\n1246|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->redy, XYZ->red_Y, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1247|      return 1;\n1248|   dwhite = d;\n1249|   whiteX = XYZ->red_X;\n1250|   whiteY = XYZ->red_Y;\n1251|\n1252|   d = XYZ->green_X + XYZ->green_Y + XYZ->green_Z;\n1253|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->greenx, XYZ->green_X, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1254|      return 1;\n1255|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->greeny, XYZ->green_Y, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1256|      return 1;\n1257|   dwhite += d;\n1258|   whiteX += XYZ->green_X;\n1259|   whiteY += XYZ->green_Y;\n1260|\n1261|   d = XYZ->blue_X + XYZ->blue_Y + XYZ->blue_Z;\n1262|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->bluex, XYZ->blue_X, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1263|      return 1;\n1264|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->bluey, XYZ->blue_Y, PNG_FP_1, d) == 0)\n1265|      return 1;\n1266|   dwhite += d;\n1267|   whiteX += XYZ->blue_X;\n1268|   whiteY += XYZ->blue_Y;\n1269|\n1270|   /* The reference white is simply the sum of the end-point (X,Y,Z) vectors,\n1271|    * thus:\n1272|    */\n1273|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->whitex, whiteX, PNG_FP_1, dwhite) == 0)\n1274|      return 1;\n1275|   if (png_muldiv(&xy->whitey, whiteY, PNG_FP_1, dwhite) == 0)\n1276|      return 1;\n1277|\n1278|   return 0;\n1279|}\n1280|\n1281|static int\n1282|png_XYZ_from_xy(png_XYZ *XYZ, const png_xy *xy)\n1283|{\n1284|   png_fixed_point red_inverse, green_inverse, blue_scale;\n1285|   png_fixed_point left, right, denominator;\n1286|\n1287|   /* Check xy and, implicitly, z.  Note that wide gamut color spaces typically\n1288|    * have end points with 0 tristimulus values (these are impossible end\n1289|    * points, but they are used to cover the possible colors).  We check\n1290|    * xy->whitey against 5, not 0, to avoid a possible integer overflow.\n1291|    */\n1292|   if (xy->redx   < 0 || xy->redx > PNG_FP_1) return 1;\n1293|   if (xy->redy   < 0 || xy->redy > PNG_FP_1-xy->redx) return 1;\n1294|   if (xy->greenx < 0 || xy->greenx > PNG_FP_1) return 1;\n1295|   if (xy->greeny < 0 || xy->greeny > PNG_FP_1-xy->greenx) return 1;\n1296|   if (xy->bluex  < 0 || xy->bluex > PNG_FP_1) return 1;\n1297|   if (xy->bluey  < 0 || xy->bluey > PNG_FP_1-xy->bluex) return 1;\n1298|   if (xy->whitex < 0 || xy->whitex > PNG_FP_1) return 1;\n1299|   if (xy->whitey < 5 || xy->whitey > PNG_FP_1-xy->whitex) return 1;\n1300|\n1301|   /* The reverse calculation is more difficult because the original tristimulus\n1302|    * value had 9 independent values (red,green,blue)x(X,Y,Z) however only 8\n1303|    * derived values were recorded in the cHRM chunk;\n1304|    * (red,green,blue,white)x(x,y).  This loses one degree of freedom and\n1305|    * therefore an arbitrary ninth value has to be introduced to undo the\n1306|    * original transformations.\n1307|    *\n1308|    * Think of the original end-points as points in (X,Y,Z) space.  The\n1309|    * chromaticity values (c) have the property:\n1310|    *\n1311|    *           C\n1312|    *   c = ---------\n1313|    *       X + Y + Z\n1314|    *\n1315|    * For each c (x,y,z) from the corresponding original C (X,Y,Z).  Thus the\n1316|    * three chromaticity values (x,y,z) for each end-point obey the\n1317|    * relationship:\n1318|    *\n1319|    *   x + y + z = 1\n1320|    *\n1321|    * This describes the plane in (X,Y,Z) space that intersects each axis at the\n1322|    * value 1.0; call this the chromaticity plane.  Thus the chromaticity\n1323|    * calculation has scaled each end-point so that it is on the x+y+z=1 plane\n1324|    * and chromaticity is the intersection of the vector from the origin to the\n1325|    * (X,Y,Z) value with the chromaticity plane.\n1326|    *\n1327|    * To fully invert the chromaticity calculation we would need the three\n1328|    * end-point scale factors, (red-scale, green-scale, blue-scale), but these\n1329|    * were not recorded.  Instead we calculated the reference white (X,Y,Z) and\n1330|    * recorded the chromaticity of this.  The reference white (X,Y,Z) would have\n1331|    * given all three of the scale factors since:\n1332|    *\n1333|    *    color-C = color-c * color-scale\n1334|    *    white-C = red-C + green-C + blue-C\n1335|    *            = red-c*red-scale + green-c*green-scale + blue-c*blue-scale\n1336|    *\n1337|    * But cHRM records only white-x and white-y, so we have lost the white scale\n1338|    * factor:\n1339|    *\n1340|    *    white-C = white-c*white-scale\n1341|    *\n1342|    * To handle this the inverse transformation makes an arbitrary assumption\n1343|    * about white-scale:\n1344|    *\n1345|    *    Assume: white-Y = 1.0\n1346|    *    Hence:  white-scale = 1/white-y\n1347|    *    Or:     red-Y + green-Y + blue-Y = 1.0\n1348|    *\n1349|    * Notice the last statement of the assumption gives an equation in three of\n1350|    * the nine values we want to calculate.  8 more equations come from the\n1351|    * above routine as summarised at the top above (the chromaticity\n1352|    * calculation):\n1353|    *\n1354|    *    Given: color-x = color-X / (color-X + color-Y + color-Z)\n1355|    *    Hence: (color-x - 1)*color-X + color.x*color-Y + color.x*color-Z = 0\n1356|    *\n1357|    * This is 9 simultaneous equations in the 9 variables \"color-C\" and can be\n1358|    * solved by Cramer's rule.  Cramer's rule requires calculating 10 9x9 matrix\n1359|    * determinants, however this is not as bad as it seems because only 28 of\n1360|    * the total of 90 terms in the various matrices are non-zero.  Nevertheless\n1361|    * Cramer's rule is notoriously numerically unstable because the determinant\n1362|    * calculation involves the difference of large, but similar, numbers.  It is\n1363|    * difficult to be sure that the calculation is stable for real world values\n1364|    * and it is certain that it becomes unstable where the end points are close\n1365|    * together.\n1366|    *\n1367|    * So this code uses the perhaps slightly less optimal but more\n1368|    * understandable and totally obvious approach of calculating color-scale.\n1369|    *\n1370|    * This algorithm depends on the precision in white-scale and that is\n1371|    * (1/white-y), so we can immediately see that as white-y approaches 0 the\n1372|    * accuracy inherent in the cHRM chunk drops off substantially.\n1373|    *\n1374|    * libpng arithmetic: a simple inversion of the above equations\n1375|    * ------------------------------------------------------------\n1376|    *\n1377|    *    white_scale = 1/white-y\n1378|    *    white-X = white-x * white-scale\n1379|    *    white-Y = 1.0\n1380|    *    white-Z = (1 - white-x - white-y) * white_scale\n1381|    *\n1382|    *    white-C = red-C + green-C + blue-C\n1383|    *            = red-c*red-scale + green-c*green-scale + blue-c*blue-scale\n1384|    *\n1385|    * This gives us three equations in (red-scale,green-scale,blue-scale) where\n1386|    * all the coefficients are now known:\n1387|    *\n1388|    *    red-x*red-scale + green-x*green-scale + blue-x*blue-scale\n1389|    *       = white-x/white-y\n1390|    *    red-y*red-scale + green-y*green-scale + blue-y*blue-scale = 1\n1391|    *    red-z*red-scale + green-z*green-scale + blue-z*blue-scale\n1392|    *       = (1 - white-x - white-y)/white-y\n1393|    *\n1394|    * In the last equation color-z is (1 - color-x - color-y) so we can add all\n1395|    * three equations together to get an alternative third:\n1396|    *\n1397|    *    red-scale + green-scale + blue-scale = 1/white-y = white-scale\n1398|    *\n1399|    * So now we have a Cramer's rule solution where the determinants are just\n1400|    * 3x3 - far more tractible.  Unfortunately 3x3 determinants still involve\n1401|    * multiplication of three coefficients so we can't guarantee to avoid\n1402|    * overflow in the libpng fixed point representation.  Using Cramer's rule in\n1403|    * floating point is probably a good choice here, but it's not an option for\n1404|    * fixed point.  Instead proceed to simplify the first two equations by\n1405|    * eliminating what is likely to be the largest value, blue-scale:\n1406|    *\n1407|    *    blue-scale = white-scale - red-scale - green-scale\n1408|    *\n1409|    * Hence:\n1410|    *\n1411|    *    (red-x - blue-x)*red-scale + (green-x - blue-x)*green-scale =\n1412|    *                (white-x - blue-x)*white-scale\n1413|    *\n1414|    *    (red-y - blue-y)*red-scale + (green-y - blue-y)*green-scale =\n1415|    *                1 - blue-y*white-scale\n1416|    *\n1417|    * And now we can trivially solve for (red-scale,green-scale):\n1418|    *\n1419|    *    green-scale =\n1420|    *                (white-x - blue-x)*white-scale - (red-x - blue-x)*red-scale\n1421|    *                -----------------------------------------------------------\n1422|    *                                  green-x - blue-x\n1423|    *\n1424|    *    red-scale =\n1425|    *                1 - blue-y*white-scale - (green-y - blue-y) * green-scale\n1426|    *                ---------------------------------------------------------\n1427|    *                                  red-y - blue-y\n1428|    *\n1429|    * Hence:\n1430|    *\n1431|    *    red-scale =\n1432|    *          ( (green-x - blue-x) * (white-y - blue-y) -\n1433|    *            (green-y - blue-y) * (white-x - blue-x) ) / white-y\n1434|    * -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1435|    *  (green-x - blue-x)*(red-y - blue-y)-(green-y - blue-y)*(red-x - blue-x)\n1436|    *\n1437|    *    green-scale =\n1438|    *          ( (red-y - blue-y) * (white-x - blue-x) -\n1439|    *            (red-x - blue-x) * (white-y - blue-y) ) / white-y\n1440|    * -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1441|    *  (green-x - blue-x)*(red-y - blue-y)-(green-y - blue-y)*(red-x - blue-x)\n1442|    *\n1443|    * Accuracy:\n1444|    * The input values have 5 decimal digits of accuracy.  The values are all in\n1445|    * the range 0 < value < 1, so simple products are in the same range but may\n1446|    * need up to 10 decimal digits to preserve the original precision and avoid\n1447|    * underflow.  Because we are using a 32-bit signed representation we cannot\n1448|    * match this; the best is a little over 9 decimal digits, less than 10.\n1449|    *\n1450|    * The approach used here is to preserve the maximum precision within the\n1451|    * signed representation.  Because the red-scale calculation above uses the\n1452|    * difference between two products of values that must be in the range -1..+1\n1453|    * it is sufficient to divide the product by 7; ceil(100,000/32767*2).  The\n1454|    * factor is irrelevant in the calculation because it is applied to both\n1455|    * numerator and denominator.\n1456|    *\n1457|    * Note that the values of the differences of the products of the\n1458|    * chromaticities in the above equations tend to be small, for example for\n1459|    * the sRGB chromaticities they are:\n1460|    *\n1461|    * red numerator:    -0.04751\n1462|    * green numerator:  -0.08788\n1463|    * denominator:      -0.2241 (without white-y multiplication)\n1464|    *\n1465|    *  The resultant Y coefficients from the chromaticities of some widely used\n1466|    *  color space definitions are (to 15 decimal places):\n1467|    *\n1468|    *  sRGB\n1469|    *    0.212639005871510 0.715168678767756 0.072192315360734\n1470|    *  Kodak ProPhoto\n1471|    *    0.288071128229293 0.711843217810102 0.000085653960605\n1472|    *  Adobe RGB\n1473|    *    0.297344975250536 0.627363566255466 0.075291458493998\n1474|    *  Adobe Wide Gamut RGB\n1475|    *    0.258728243040113 0.724682314948566 0.016589442011321\n1476|    */\n1477|   /* By the argument, above overflow should be impossible here. The return\n1478|    * value of 2 indicates an internal error to the caller.\n1479|    */\n1480|   if (png_muldiv(&left, xy->greenx-xy->bluex, xy->redy - xy->bluey, 7) == 0)\n1481|      return 2;\n1482|   if (png_muldiv(&right, xy->greeny-xy->bluey, xy->redx - xy->bluex, 7) == 0)\n1483|      return 2;\n1484|   denominator = left - right;\n1485|\n1486|   /* Now find the red numerator. */\n1487|   if (png_muldiv(&left, xy->greenx-xy->bluex, xy->whitey-xy->bluey, 7) == 0)\n1488|      return 2;\n1489|   if (png_muldiv(&right, xy->greeny-xy->bluey, xy->whitex-xy->bluex, 7) == 0)\n1490|      return 2;\n1491|\n1492|   /* Overflow is possible here and it indicates an extreme set of PNG cHRM\n1493|    * chunk values.  This calculation actually returns the reciprocal of the\n1494|    * scale value because this allows us to delay the multiplication of white-y\n1495|    * into the denominator, which tends to produce a small number.\n1496|    */\n1497|   if (png_muldiv(&red_inverse, xy->whitey, denominator, left-right) == 0 ||\n1498|       red_inverse <= xy->whitey /* r+g+b scales = white scale */)\n1499|      return 1;\n1500|\n1501|   /* Similarly for green_inverse: */\n1502|   if (png_muldiv(&left, xy->redy-xy->bluey, xy->whitex-xy->bluex, 7) == 0)\n1503|      return 2;\n1504|   if (png_muldiv(&right, xy->redx-xy->bluex, xy->whitey-xy->bluey, 7) == 0)\n1505|      return 2;\n1506|   if (png_muldiv(&green_inverse, xy->whitey, denominator, left-right) == 0 ||\n1507|       green_inverse <= xy->whitey)\n1508|      return 1;\n1509|\n1510|   /* And the blue scale, the checks above guarantee this can't overflow but it\n1511|    * can still produce 0 for extreme cHRM values.\n1512|    */\n1513|   blue_scale = png_reciprocal(xy->whitey) - png_reciprocal(red_inverse) -\n1514|       png_reciprocal(green_inverse);\n1515|   if (blue_scale <= 0)\n1516|      return 1;\n1517|\n1518|\n1519|   /* And fill in the png_XYZ: */\n1520|   if (png_muldiv(&XYZ->red_X, xy->redx, PNG_FP_1, red_inverse) == 0)\n1521|      return 1;\n1522|   if (png_muldiv(&XYZ->red_Y, xy->redy, PNG_FP_1, red_inverse) == 0)\n1523|      return 1;\n1524|   if (png_muldiv(&XYZ->red_Z, PNG_FP_1 - xy->redx - xy->redy, PNG_FP_1,\n1525|       red_inverse) == 0)\n1526|      return 1;\n1527|\n1528|   if 1;\n1532|   if (png_muldiv(&XYZ->green_Z, PNG_FP_1 - xy->greenx - xy->greeny, PNG_FP_1,\n1533|       green_inverse) == 0)\n1534|      return 1;\n1535|\n1536|   if (png_muldiv(&XYZ->blue_X, xy->bluex, xy->bluex - xy->bluey, blue_scale,\n1541|       PNG_FP_1) == 0)\n1542|      return 1;\n1543|\n1544|   return 0; /*success*/\n1545|}\n1546|\n1547|static int\n1548|png_XYZ_normalize(png_XYZ XYZ->red_Z < 0 || XYZ->green_Z < 0 || XYZ->blue_Z < 0)\n1555|      return 1;\n1556|\n1557|   /* Normalize by scaling so the sum of the end-point Y values is PNG_FP_1.\n1558|    * IMPLEMENTATION NOTE: Despite this it is\n1617| * theoretically possible to produce chromaticities that are apparently valid\n1618| * but that rapidly degrade to invalid, potentially crashing, sets because of\n1619| * arithmetic inaccuracies when calculations are performed on them.  The new\n1620| * check is to round-trip xy -> XYZ -> xy and then check that the result is\n1621| * within a small percentage of the original.\n1622| */\n1623|static int\n1624|png_colorspace_check_xy(png_XYZ *XYZ, const png_xy *xy)\n1625|{\n1626|   int result;\n1627|   png_xy xy_test;\n1628|\n1629|   /* As a side-effect this routine also returns the XYZ endpoints. */\n1630|   result = png_XYZ_from_xy(XYZ, xy);\n1631|   if (result != 0)\n1632|      return result;\n1633|\n1634|   result = png_xy_from_XYZ(&xy_test, XYZ);\n1635|   if (result != 0)\n1636|      return result;\n1637|\n1638|   if (png_colorspace_endpoints_match(xy, &xy_test,\n1639|       5/*actually, the math is pretty accurate*/) != 0)\n1640|      return 0;\n1641|\n1642|   /* Too much slip */\n1643|   return 1;\n1644|}\n1645|\n1646|/* This is the check going the other way. */\n3297|            }\n3298|         }\n3299|         else\n3300|            *ascii++ = 48;\n3301|\n3302|         /* And null terminate the string: */\n3303|         *ascii = 0;\n3304|         return;\n3305|      }\n3306|   }\n3307|\n3308|   /* Here on buffer too small. */\n3309|   png_error(png_ptr, \"ASCII conversion buffer too small\");\n3310|}\n3311|#   endif /* FIXED_POINT */\n3312|#endif /* SCAL */\n3313|\n3314|#if defined(PNG_FLOATING_POINT_SUPPORTED) && \\\n3315|   !defined(PNG_FIXED_POINT_MACRO_SUPPORTED) && \\\n3316|   (defined(PNG_gAMA_SUPPORTED) || defined(PNG_cHRM_SUPPORTED) || \\\n3317|   defined(PNG_sCAL_SUPPORTED) || defined(PNG_READ_BACKGROUND_SUPPORTED) || \\\n3318|   defined(PNG_READ_RGB_TO_GRAY_SUPPORTED)) || \\\n3319|   (defined(PNG_sCAL_SUPPORTED) && \\\n3320|   defined(PNG_FLOATING_ARITHMETIC_SUPPORTED))\n3321|png_fixed_point\n3322|png_fixed(png_const_structrp png_ptr, double fp, png_const_charp text)\n3323|{\n3324|   double r = floor(100000 * fp + .5);\n3325|\n3326|   if (r > 2147483647. || r < -2147483648.)\n3327|      png_fixed_error(png_ptr, text);\n3328|\n3329|#  ifndef PNG_ERROR_TEXT_SUPPORTED\n3330|   PNG_UNUSED(text)\n3331|#  endif\n3332|\n3333|   return (png_fixed_point)r;\n3334|}\n3335|#endif\n3336|\n3337|#if defined(PNG_GAMMA_SUPPORTED) || defined(PNG_COLORSPACE_SUPPORTED) ||\\\n3338|    defined(PNG_INCH_CONVERSIONS_SUPPORTED) || defined(PNG_READ_pHYs_SUPPORTED)\n3339|/* muldiv functions */\n3340|/* This API takes signed arguments and rounds the result to the nearest\n3341| * integer (or, for a fixed point number - the standard argument - to\n3342| * the nearest .00001).  Overflow and divide by zero are signalled in\n3343| * the result, a boolean - true on success, false on overflow.\n3344| */\n3345|#if GCC_STRICT_OVERFLOW /* from above */\n3346|/* It is not obvious Return a * times / divisor, rounded. */\n3359|   if (divisor != 0)\n3360|   {\n3361|      if (a == 0 || times == 0)\n3362|      {\n3363|         *res = 0;\n3364|         return 1;\n3365|      }\n3366|      else\n3367|      {\n3368|#ifdef PNG_FLOATING_ARITHMETIC_SUPPORTED\n3369|         double r = a;\n3370|         r *= times;\n3371|         r /= divisor;\n3372|         r = The final\n3650|    * returned value thus has the range 0 (for 255 input) to 7.994 (for 1\n3651|    * input), return -1 for the overflow (log 0) case, - so the result is\n3652|    * always at most 19 bits.\n3653|    */\n3654|   if ((x &= 0xff) == 0)\n3655|      return -1;\n3656|\n3657|   if ((x & 0xf0) == 0)\n3658|      lg2  = 4, x <<= 4;\n3659|\n3660|   if ((x & 0xc0) == 0)\n3661|      lg2 += 2, x <<= 2;\n3662|\n3663|   if ((x & 0x80) == 0)\n3664|      lg2 += 1, x <<= 1;\n3665|\n3666|   /* result is at most 19 bits, so this cast is safe: */\n3667|   return (png_int_32)((lg2 << 16) + ((png_8bit_l2[x-128]+32768)>>16));\n3668|}\n3669|\n3670|/* The above gives exact (to 16 binary places) log2 values for 8-bit images,\n3671| * for 16-bit images we use the most significant 8 bits of the 16-bit value to\n3672| * get an approximation then multiply the approximation by a correction factor\n3673| * determined by the remaining up to 8 bits.  This requires an additional step\n3674| * in the 16-bit case.\n3675| *\n3676| * We want log2(value/65535), we have log2(v'/255), where:\n3677| *\n3678| *    value = v' * 256 + v''\n3679| *          = v' * f\n3680| *\n3681| * So f is value/v', which is equal to (256+v''/v') since v' is in the range 128\n3682| * to 255 and v'' is in the range 0 to 255 f will be in the range 256 to less\n3683| * than 258.  The final factor also needs to correct for the fact that our 8-bit\n3684| * value is scaled by 255, whereas the 16-bit values must be scaled by 65535.\n3685| *\n3686| * This gives a final formula using a calculated value 'x' which is value/v' and\n3687| * scaling by 65536 to match the above table:\n3688| *\n3689| *   log2(x/257) * 65536\n3690| *\n3691| * Since these numbers are so close to '1' we can use simple linear\n3692| * interpolation between the two end values 256/257 (result -368.61) and 258/257\n3693| * (result 367.179).  The values used below are scaled by a further 64 to give\n3694| * 16-bit precision in the interpolation:\n3695| *\n3696| * Start (256): -23591\n3697| * Zero  (257):      0\n3698| * End   (258):  23499\n3699| */\n3700|#ifdef PNG_16BIT_SUPPORTED\n3701|static png_int_32\n3702|png_log16bit(png_uint_32 x)\n3703|{\n3704|   unsigned int lg2 = 0;\n3705|\n3706|   /* As above, but now the input has 16 bits. */\n3707|   if ((x &= 0xffff) == 0)\n3708|      return -1;\n3709|\n3710|   if ((x & 0xff00) == 0)\n3711|      lg2  = 8, x <<= 8;\n3712|\n3713|   if ((x & 0xf000) == 0)\n3714|      lg2 += 4, x <<= 4;\n3715|\n3716|   if ((x & 0xc000) == 0)\n3717|      lg2 += 2, x <<= 2;\n3718|\n3719|   if ((x & 0x8000) == 0)\n3720|      lg2 += 1, x <<= 1;\n3721|\n3722|   /* Calculate the base logarithm from the top 8 bits as a 28-bit fractional\n3723|    * value.\n3724|    */\n3725|   lg2 <<= 28;\n3726|   lg2 += (png_8bit_l2[(x>>8)-128]+8) >> 4;\n3727|\n3728|   /* Now we need to interpolate the factor, this requires a division by the top\n3729|    * 8 bits.  Do this with maximum precision.\n3730|    */\n3731|   x = ((x << 16) + (x >> 9)) / (x >> 8);\n3732|\n3733|   /* Since we divided by the top 8 bits of 'x' there will be a '1' at 1<<24,\n3734|    * the value at 1<<16 (ignoring this) will be 0 or 1; this gives us exactly\n3735|    * 16 bits to interpolate to get the low bits of the result.  Round the\n3736|    * answer.  Note that the end point values are scaled by 64 to retain overall\n3737|    * precision and that 'lg2' is current scaled by an extra 12 bits, so adjust\n3738|    * the overall scaling by 6-12.  Round at every step.\n3739|    */\n3740|   x -= 1U << 24;\n3741|\n3742|   if (x <= 65536U) /* <= '257' */\n3743|      lg2 += ((23591U * (65536U-x)) + (1U << (16+6-12-1))) >> (16+6-12);\n3744|\n3745|   else\n3746|      lg2 -= ((23499U * (x-65536U)) + (1U << (16+6-12-1))) >> (16+6-12);\n3747|\n3748|   /* Safe, because the result can't have more than 20 bits: */\n3749|   return (png_int_32)((lg2 + 2048) >> 12);\n3750|}\n3751|#endif /* 16BIT */\n3752|\n3753|/* The 'exp()' case must invert the above, taking a 20-bit fixed point\n3754| * logarithmic value and returning a 16 or 8-bit number as appropriate.  In\n3755| * each case only the low 16 bits are relevant - the fraction - since the\n3756| * integer bits (the top 4) simply determine a shift.\n3757| *\n3758| * The worst case is the 16-bit distinction between 65535 and 65534. This\n3759| * requires perhaps spurious accuracy in the decoding of the logarithm to\n3760| * distinguish log2(65535/65534.5) - 10^-5 or 17 bits.  There is little chance\n3761| * of getting this accuracy in practice.\n3762| *\n3763| * To deal with this the following exp() function works out the exponent of the\n3764| * frational part of the logarithm by using an accurate 32-bit value from the\n3765| * top four fractional bits then multiplying in the remaining bits.\n3766| */\n3767|static const png_uint_32\n3768|png_32bit_exp[16] =\n3769|{\n3770|   /* NOTE: the first entry is deliberately set to the maximum 32-bit value. */\n3771|   4294967295U, 4112874773U, 3938502376U, 3771522796U, 3611622603U, 3458501653U,\n3772|   3311872529U, 3171459999U, 3037000500U, 2908241642U, 2784941738U, 2666869345U,\n3773|   2553802834U, 2445529972U, 2341847524U, 2242560872U\n3774|};\n3775|\n3776|/* Adjustment table; provided to explain the numbers in the code below. */\n3777|#if 0\n3778|for (i=11;i>=0;--i){ print i, \" \", (1 - e(-(2^i)/65536*l(2))) * 2^(32-i), \"\\n\"}\n3779|   11 44937.64284865548751208448\n3780|   10 45180.98734845585101160448\n3781|    9 45303.31936980687359311872\n3782|    8 45364.65110595323018870784\n3783|    7 45395.35850361789624614912\n3784|    6 45410.72259715102037508096\n3785|    5 45418.40724413220722311168\n3786|    4 45422.25021786898173001728\n3787|    3 45424.17186732298419044352\n3788|    2 45425.13273269940811464704\n3789|    1 45425.61317555035558641664\n3790|    0 45425.85339951654943850496\n3791|#endif\n3792|\n3793|static png_uint_32\n3794|png_exp(png_fixed_point x)\n3795|{\n3796|   if (x > 0 && x <= 0xfffff) /* Else overflow or zero (underflow) */\n3797|   {\n3798|      /* Obtain a 4-bit approximation */\n3799|      png_uint_32 e = png_32bit_exp[(x >> 12) & 0x0f];\n3800|\n3801|      /* Incorporate the low 12 bits - these decrease the returned value by\n3802|       * multiplying by a number less than 1 if the bit is set. But because we are pretending\n333|     * that most of the images originate on the Internet, we follow the recom-\n334|     * mendation of the sRGB proposal and treat unlabelled images (no gAMA\n335|     * chunk) as existing in the sRGB color space.
